# AMA-S — whole synthesis and its public disclosure boundary

> **Current scientific edition — 13 November 2026.** Read the [substantive current research](current/AMA-S.md) for the public scientific account, corrected predicates, guards, contrary findings and evidence limits. The record below is the earlier documentary cutoff. Its generic PENDING/private/no-public-body statements are dated history and do not describe the current edition. Historical scientific bodies and their original source identities remain unchanged.

## Earlier documentary record

## Current qualification and exact public scope

The independently received **AMA-S r18 A002** is qualified at its declared scientific scope on subject SHA-256 `4fd89fbb8867b55788cf083523a6546b3ded8e515567de85b0db2e548377232e`. Both final reviews accept that same subject; exact qualification and review identities are in [SOURCE_MAP.json](SOURCE_MAP.json). Detailed current interpretations remain private pending **AMA-DISCLOSE-AMA-S**. The staged central intent has zero effects and a null commit; qualification does not itself prove central application.

This public account explains the **already-public September synthesis**, revision `AMA-2026-09-07-r1`, rather than mislabelling its findings as the November accepted result. The full current scientific account, its changed directional judgments, adverse history, all selected records and both actual independent reviews are retained privately. The historical r17 `GAP_REVISE`, older partial null and r18 final qualification remain distinct. The full public account of current science is not complete while that disclosure dependency remains.

## A genuine guarded A × B × C account

A is the individual agent's observation, goal, action, effect and bounded revision. B is the inter-party dependency, public meaning, obligation, coordination or collective consequence. C is the justified engineering requirement, realization, evidence, acceptance and responsible change. A useful joint result needs actual applicable contributions at the claimed bearer and context. Adding three lists, agreeing on terminology or counting source overlap does not establish the resulting criterion. [Historical frozen report](https://github.com/theislampill/IMPLEMENTAUDIT.md/blob/cacfe97da7656b472bf4f11e50c9d106a6c3f92a/docs/research/genealogy/ama/packet/EVOLVED_AMA_FROZEN_PACKET.zip)

The predicates remain distinct: an intention can be dropped while a public promise remains; capability can exist without permission; a request can be accepted without being performed; an observed effect can fail the beneficiary's acceptance criterion. For a particular dispatch, the material warrants must refer to the compatible action/actor/configuration/time. For acceptance, evidence must establish the actual receiving criterion. An authorized experiment can proceed on warranted predictions without pretending its future result already exists. An adequate atomic interface may satisfy a small join with little machinery.

One historical directional example is **EAA-003 → EMAS-009**: a justified private mental-state communication model requires implemented semantics before its private-state assumptions can be assessed. A prompt label alone does not implement those semantics. The source does not assert an automatic reciprocal direction of the same type. Another example, **EAA-004 → EMAS-004**, reinforces the distinction between individual capability/permission and collective information/control/authority when an actor joins a collective undertaking. Both are guarded uses; neither creates native execution authority or an inferred scheduler edge. All 105 historical bridges are reproduced below with their actual endpoints, type, basis, guard and omission consequence.

## Higher-order conclusions, counterexamples and non-entailments

The historical triangle uses x=y, x=z and y≠z. Every proper pair of constraints admits a witness, but all three have no joint witness. The conclusion is a real higher-order failure possibility: separately satisfactory local or pairwise views cannot establish global feasibility. The stronger inherited compatible-support and global-acceptance criteria already reject the example, so it does not establish a distinct new property. A related epoch example has individually valid receipts that cannot support one common current action.

Cost must also be composed at its real scope. Three disjoint four-unit controls exceed a ten-unit budget, while one shared four-unit operation must not be counted three times. Incomparable burdens do not acquire a universal scalar conversion. More coordination can consume the opportunity to act; fewer mechanisms can omit a necessary protection. The adequate alternative must meet the same actual information, rights and outcome constraints.

Historical counterexamples also preserve: a live promise after local intention retirement; an incapable or unauthorized performer after a valid award; inaccessible evidence; repair that consumes another team's promised resource; role reconfiguration that loses an obligation; a proxy improved by changing the protected objective; late human approval; copied memory mistaken for independent support; and an ordinary implementation that satisfies the need without agents. These are finite analytical constructions, not empirical failure rates. All 24 source records remain individually addressable below.

## Alternatives and substantive limits

The fifteen historical configuration branches include conventional implementation, AO analysis with conventional execution, single reactive or deliberative agents, hybrids, cooperative teams, public commitments, role organizations, adaptive organizations, mixed human-agent work, bounded higher assurance, generative systems, strategic allocation, distributed constraints and collective decision support. They are alternatives with conditional mechanisms and retirement paths. BDI, FIPA, public commitments, a human approval loop, a generative model and persistent memory are not universal ingredients.

The historical novelty search rejected twenty hypotheses and admitted zero new AMA-E identities. Existing EAA/EMAS derived rows remain inherited. Relation bundles, failure witnesses and conditional configurations remain useful outcomes even without a new property. Rejected general self-revision assurance is unsupported, not proved impossible. General self-revision, current FIPA/runtime equivalence and the two separate AOSE whole-benefit questions remain unresolved at their recorded historical scope.

The **historical** synthesis accounts for 24,592 cross-lineage pairs: 9,178 material and 15,414 with no established material relation under its recorded rules; 20,152 directional records; 54 semantic profiles; 105 manual bridges; 32 clusters; sixteen bundles; 24 tensions; 24 counterexamples; fifteen alternatives; and twenty rejected novelty candidates. These are neither the current r18 numerical judgments nor independent empirical results. “No material relation” at that freeze is not universal independence. Source repetition is not independent corroboration.

## Separate native receiving and responsibility

Published, canonically unallocated **R0043::AMA-CONTINUATION / #232** is the existing historical 278-original/32-group native documentary carrier. It does not add a fourth umbrella controller, close a constituent scope by naming it, or qualify a new autonomous runtime. The public predecessor's parent remains accountable for integration and preserves human/owner effect boundaries while receiving bounded child evidence. [Owner explanation](OWNER_COMPOSITION.md) · [Existing native qualification](../../../../../implementaudit/r0043-eaose-ama-qualification.md)

The whole-family rough responsibility asks whether an exact-current composition claim is warranted across actual native owners and receiving consumers. A positive constituent receipt is necessary evidence for some joins but is not by itself sufficient for a whole result. Existing governors, dependency joins, scoped lanes, lifecycle composition and systems-interface owners remain separately accountable. No new issue, native delta, merge or deployment is authorized by these outlines.

The sections below transcribe source-specific historical records. Their complete nested primary-source and original-constituent context remains in the immutable source packet; current protected corrections are preserved in the separate private account. This is a bounded public historical projection with complete record populations, not a replacement for the current accepted scientific subject.

### Q1 — Consequential goal–authority–obligation–effect join

**Question:** Q1

**Title:** Consequential goal–authority–obligation–effect join

**Answer:** EAA-067 already excludes splicing individually valid facts into a nonexistent action basis; EMAS-071 already separates evidence, obligation, intervention and consequence. Canonical EAOSE-RB01 expressly applies C086/C002/C007/C058/C098/C067 to one subject/action/configuration/time/acceptance context. Renaming that existing bundle at AMA level creates no residual criterion.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** Do not justify one consequential dispatch or acceptance by unrelated goal, scope, commitment, capability or effect records; the material bindings and claim-specific evidence must be compatible.

**Composition contribution:** Local current action basis meets public interaction/obligation semantics and engineering acceptance. The composition exposes the join obligations and the distinction between permission to attempt and evidence to accept.

### Q2 — Cross-level intention and obligation continuity

**Question:** Q2

**Title:** Cross-level intention and obligation continuity

**Answer:** C105 explicitly requires retaining, validly transferring, releasing, compensating or reporting inability for outstanding public obligations at reassignment; C051 already asks what happened to dependent obligations when intention changes. MAS persistence/alignment supplies the social mechanics. This is a new cross-level application, not a new property.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** A private change of intention must not silently discharge, orphan or transfer a public obligation or persisting organisational responsibility.

**Composition contribution:** Distinguish the local intention lifecycle from the public commitment lifecycle and the valid organisational transition between responsible principals.

### Q3 — Delegation realised by an authorised capable executor

**Question:** Q3

**Title:** Delegation realised by an authorised capable executor

**Answer:** C007 is already the exact practical-ability/current-permission point-of-use criterion. EMAS-004/015/016 and EAA-004/021 distinguish its components. Adding the phrase delegation realisation is not a distinct bearer/criterion beyond those inherited requirements.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** A legitimate request, selected worker and role assignment must not be accepted as fulfilment unless the actual actor has the required capability and current permission, and performance is separately evidenced.

**Composition contribution:** Join authority for delegation with task-allocation selection and the individual action interface; distinguish acceptance of an award from execution.

### Q5 — Public compliance without forced private-state inspection

**Question:** Q5

**Title:** Public compliance without forced private-state inspection

**Answer:** EMAS-008 already supplies the opaque-participant public-effect criterion; EMAS-009 and C037 retain a guarded private-state branch. ### Q7 — Epistemic reachability of acceptance evidence

**Question:** Q7

**Title:** Epistemic reachability of acceptance evidence

**Answer:** C106 states the proposed criterion directly, including reachability, authority, capability, usability and legitimate omission. Calling it epistemic reachability changes terminology, not its property identity. Information privacy may make the condition infeasible; that is an exposed configuration failure, not an assurance theorem.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** Evidence needed by a consequential acceptance or intervention must be available in usable form to an authorised actor capable of making that decision in time, or the claim remains open.

**Composition contribution:** Distinguish local observation adequacy and distributed provenance from the information, authority and practical ability of the actual consequential consumer.

### Q15 — Global compatibility beyond pairwise consistency

**Question:** Q15

**Title:** Global compatibility beyond pairwise consistency

**Answer:** The example proves a higher-order failure possibility, not novelty. C086 already requires a composed configuration to supply compatible support; C079 already rejects compatibility-to-achievability. The full C061/EMAS-020 criteria do not pass globally just because their pairwise projections pass. No independent property remains.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** Do not infer a globally compatible configuration or acceptance warrant solely from separately satisfiable pairwise projections.

**Composition contribution:** Construct a finite triangle with x=y, x=z and y≠z: every proper constraint subset has a witness, but the full join is empty. Test the resulting global claim against already inherited compatible-support and actual feasibility criteria.

### Q16 — Composed control-cost and retirement envelope

**Question:** Q16

**Title:** Composed control-cost and retirement envelope

**Answer:** Whole-task and lifecycle accounting already include displaced burdens, and minimum adequate coordination/method retirement already permit simplification while preserving obligations. The 4+4+4&gt;10 example exposes an omitted global budget, not a new criterion beyond those inherited.

**Derived joint requirement or boundary:** Individually useful reasoning, coordination and assurance must still fit the actual whole-task/lifecycle cost and obligation envelope; excess machinery may be simplified or retired.

**Composition contribution:** Bring local reasoning cost, collective communication/integration burden and engineering lifecycle effort into the same declared comparison without assuming one universal additive scalar.

## All 24 historical tensions

These are the September public source records, not the corrected r18 scientific findings. Source: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions` in the [immutable historical packet](https://github.com/theislampill/IMPLEMENTAUDIT.md/blob/cacfe97da7656b472bf4f11e50c9d106a6c3f92a/docs/research/genealogy/ama/packet/EVOLVED_AMA_FROZEN_PACKET.zip).

### AMA-T001 — Autonomy versus legitimate authority

**Id:** AMA-T001

**Name:** Autonomy versus legitimate authority

**Scope a:** Discretion can handle contingencies when the task and delegated choice space justify it.

**Scope b:** Legitimate principals and affected-party constraints bound which consequences an actor may cause.

**Failure if a universalised:** Technical ability or local goal value is mistaken for a right to act.

**Failure if b universalised:** Requiring fresh approval for every harmless in-scope step destroys useful delegated discretion.

**Bounded synthesis:** Keep hard authority limits and allow conditional means selection inside them; refusal, deferral and existing constrained interfaces are legitimate. No average autonomy score substitutes for permission.

**Unresolved remainder:** The packets do not resolve every external legitimacy dispute or determine one universal confirmation granularity.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/0`.

### AMA-T002 — Local goal optimisation versus public and organisational obligations

**Id:** AMA-T002

**Name:** Local goal optimisation versus public and organisational obligations

**Scope a:** An individual should pursue an adequate goal and stop obsolete or impossible local work.

**Scope b:** Other parties can still hold valid expectations even after the individual changes intention.

**Failure if a universalised:** A locally beneficial repair or retirement silently abandons a promise.

**Failure if b universalised:** Unconditional persistence wastes resources or pursues an impossible task indefinitely.

**Bounded synthesis:** Reconsider the local pursuit, then retain, transfer, release, compensate or report the dependent obligation through its valid transition semantics.

**Unresolved remainder:** Release/transfer can be impossible when counterparties are unreachable; explicit unresolved responsibility is not fulfilment.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/1`.

### AMA-T003 — Private intentional architecture versus publicly inspectable compliance

**Id:** AMA-T003

**Name:** Private intentional architecture versus publicly inspectable compliance

**Scope a:** Mental-state semantics can constrain a justified closed/cooperative implementation and its interpreter.

**Scope b:** Open opaque participants need assessable public effects rather than assumed sincerity.

**Failure if a universalised:** An inaccessible belief or asserted intention is treated as a public compliance certificate.

**Failure if b universalised:** Forcing public commitments into every internal decision discards useful BDI reasoning and adds irrelevant bureaucracy.

**Bounded synthesis:** Separate the internal reasoning architecture from the external compliance criterion; disclose exactly where private-state assumptions are warranted.

**Unresolved remainder:** EAOSE-C037 remains contested and EMAS-009 assumption-sensitive; composition does not establish universal superiority of either semantics.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/2`.

### AMA-T004 — Decentralised control versus coordinated feasibility

**Id:** AMA-T004

**Name:** Decentralised control versus coordinated feasibility

**Scope a:** Distributed ownership, information or capabilities may make a central controller impossible or undesirable.

**Scope b:** Shared resources and collective constraints can require a jointly feasible decision or fallback.

**Failure if a universalised:** Independent locally legal actions conflict or shared services become hidden single points of failure.

**Failure if b universalised:** A universal coordinator claims knowledge or authority it does not possess and becomes a bottleneck.

**Bounded synthesis:** Choose direct, central, distributed or dependency-eliminating mechanisms against the actual control boundary and joint constraints.

**Unresolved remainder:** No universal decentralisation or central-world-model theorem is supplied; target topology and fault evidence remain configuration-specific.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/3`.

### AMA-T005 — Communication richness versus consequential cost

**Id:** AMA-T005

**Name:** Communication richness versus consequential cost

**Scope a:** Some commitments, bindings and coordination decisions require timely information with adequate semantics.

**Scope b:** Messages consume time, attention, bandwidth and privacy; more exchange may change nothing useful.

**Failure if a universalised:** Redundant dialogue delays execution and increases misunderstanding or disclosure.

**Failure if b universalised:** Cost reduction suppresses mandatory notifications or the only decision-relevant fact.

**Bounded synthesis:** Keep mandatory obligation notifications and sufficient semantic bindings; optimise discretionary communication by its consequential value.

**Unresolved remainder:** Expected value estimates and response timing can be wrong; no fixed message budget is universally adequate.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/4`.

### AMA-T006 — Fast local response versus organisation-wide consistency

**Id:** AMA-T006

**Name:** Fast local response versus organisation-wide consistency

**Scope a:** Reactive control and responsive generation waits can be necessary to meet action windows.

**Scope b:** A consequential action cannot rely on incompatible task, authority, version or shared-resource facts.

**Failure if a universalised:** Fast dispatch uses revoked authority or invalidates a teammate’s protected preconditions.

**Failure if b universalised:** Global synchronisation on every action misses deadlines or prevents useful bounded operation.

**Bounded synthesis:** Check only material dependencies and use justified atomicity, stable contracts or bounded uncertainty; retain a feasible fallback rather than a universal synchronisation barrier.

**Unresolved remainder:** Time-of-check/use races, delayed observations and acceptable staleness depend on the actual execution model.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/5`.

### AMA-T007 — Intention persistence versus reconsideration cost and relevance

**Id:** AMA-T007

**Name:** Intention persistence versus reconsideration cost and relevance

**Scope a:** Commitment supports sustained work and coordination through irrelevant distractions.

**Scope b:** Changed feasibility, relevance or priority can make continued pursuit harmful.

**Failure if a universalised:** Agents persist in obsolete plans and consume resources or violate updated scope.

**Failure if b universalised:** Continuous reconsideration destabilises joint work and uses the whole action window.

**Bounded synthesis:** Use guarded event/deadline/value-based reconsideration; distinguish goal failure from plan failure and preserve dependent obligations.

**Unresolved remainder:** The right reconsideration schedule is environment- and cost-sensitive; no universal optimum is established.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/6`.

### AMA-T008 — Flexible repair versus protocol and binding conformance

**Id:** AMA-T008

**Name:** Flexible repair versus protocol and binding conformance

**Scope a:** Alternative means, recontracting and local repair can restore useful progress.

**Scope b:** Protocols and live binding rules protect partners from unilateral reinterpretation.

**Failure if a universalised:** A repair changes the principal, task or promise while preserving only the task name.

**Failure if b universalised:** Rigid enactment rules block an authorised equivalent repair or valid renegotiation.

**Bounded synthesis:** Permit substitutions within actual equivalence and authority; renegotiate or rebind explicitly when the social meaning changes.

**Unresolved remainder:** Semantic equivalence of repaired goals and consent to altered obligations can be underdetermined.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/7`.

### AMA-T009 — Adaptive improvement versus continuity of evidence

**Id:** AMA-T009

**Name:** Adaptive improvement versus continuity of evidence

**Scope a:** Learning and structural change can address changed tasks and partners.

**Scope b:** Prior evidence is valid only while the premises supporting its claim persist.

**Failure if a universalised:** A local score improvement carries an obsolete assurance claim into a different regime.

**Failure if b universalised:** Treating every change as invalidating all evidence imposes needless full revalidation and can block needed adaptation.

**Bounded synthesis:** Trace affected premises, preserve independently valid evidence and reopen only the necessary acceptance/partner claims.

**Unresolved remainder:** Affected-premise discovery can miss hidden dependencies, and a changed premise does not logically prove the conclusion false.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/8`.

### AMA-T010 — Self-revision versus predecessor-governed acceptance

**Id:** AMA-T010

**Name:** Self-revision versus predecessor-governed acceptance

**Scope a:** Bounded policy, organisational and method revisions can be useful under declared purposes and powers.

**Scope b:** A successor’s score or rule cannot independently authorise and certify its own revision.

**Failure if a universalised:** The revision changes both the protected objective and its evaluator, then declares improvement.

**Failure if b universalised:** Absolute prohibition of every revision preserves obsolete faults and prevents authorised learning.

**Bounded synthesis:** Identify the changed object, preserve or legitimately revise protected criteria under the predecessor decision boundary, and retain external consequence evidence.

**Unresolved remainder:** EAA-062 remains unresolved; the idealised EAA-070 theorem does not establish general deployed self-revision or corrigibility.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/9`.

### AMA-T011 — Role reassignment versus outstanding obligation preservation

**Id:** AMA-T011

**Name:** Role reassignment versus outstanding obligation preservation

**Scope a:** Capabilities, membership and load can justify a new structurally valid role allocation.

**Scope b:** Pre-existing commitments have parties and valid transition rules that outlive a process assignment.

**Failure if a universalised:** The new organisation is valid but predecessor obligations disappear.

**Failure if b universalised:** Freezing all assignments until every obligation ends makes recoverable departures and failures unrecoverable.

**Bounded synthesis:** Reconcile each affected live obligation into valid retention, transfer, release, compensation or explicit inability; neither structural validity nor message emission is enough.

**Unresolved remainder:** Transfers may lack a capable willing successor; preserve inability rather than invent closure.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/10`.

### AMA-T012 — Specialisation versus substitution and replacement

**Id:** AMA-T012

**Name:** Specialisation versus substitution and replacement

**Scope a:** A specialised skill or role can supply a contribution unavailable to a generic participant.

**Scope b:** Specialisation can create bottlenecks, incompatible assumptions and an expensive replacement boundary.

**Failure if a universalised:** Many narrow specialists add handoffs and leave a unique unavailable actor as a hidden dependency.

**Failure if b universalised:** Flattening every role removes capabilities or ownership boundaries that genuinely matter.

**Bounded synthesis:** Justify each specialisation by marginal contribution and test feasible substitutions with explicit capability, role and commitment compatibility.

**Unresolved remainder:** Task distributions and specialist availability change; no universal ideal agent count follows.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/11`.

### AMA-T013 — Agent count versus total coordination and assurance burden

**Id:** AMA-T013

**Name:** Agent count versus total coordination and assurance burden

**Scope a:** Additional participants may add genuinely separate capabilities or control.

**Scope b:** Allocation, communication, integration, evaluation and supervision can grow faster than useful output.

**Failure if a universalised:** An architecture becomes slower and more expensive while duplicate agents merely repeat evidence.

**Failure if b universalised:** Fewer agents is optimised as an end in itself despite loss of necessary distributed capability.

**Bounded synthesis:** Compare the smallest adequate configurations on matched or transparently unmatched budgets and complete relevant cost dimensions.

**Unresolved remainder:** Net lifecycle benefit of the whole AMA family is not established by local component gains.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/12`.

### AMA-T014 — Delegation versus verification and trust cost

**Id:** AMA-T014

**Name:** Delegation versus verification and trust cost

**Scope a:** Legitimate delegation can place work with the appropriate actor and reduce direct supervision.

**Scope b:** Acceptance and performance still need evidence appropriate to consequence and uncertainty.

**Failure if a universalised:** A bid, reputation score or accepted assignment is mistaken for feasible execution or fulfilment.

**Failure if b universalised:** Verifying every internal step duplicates work, overloads supervisors and destroys useful delegation.

**Bounded synthesis:** Verify the material authority, capability and outcome boundaries, using adequate existing contracts and risk-limited trust rather than maximal surveillance.

**Unresolved remainder:** What a return contract establishes and how much exposure a trust signal justifies remain task-specific.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/13`.

### AMA-T015 — Cooperation assumptions versus strategic or adversarial behaviour

**Id:** AMA-T015

**Name:** Cooperation assumptions versus strategic or adversarial behaviour

**Scope a:** Shared objectives can justify inexpensive team plans and conditional persistence.

**Scope b:** Participants may have different incentives, adapt, deceive, collude or fail under a different threat model.

**Failure if a universalised:** A cooperative guarantee is applied to a strategic participant without its premises.

**Failure if b universalised:** Treating every benign teammate as maximally adversarial introduces unjustified cost and destroys useful coordination.

**Bounded synthesis:** State the regime, test relevant deviations and hand off/revalidate when assumptions change; do not infer legitimacy from equilibrium.

**Unresolved remainder:** Opponent shifts and covert collusion can remain unobserved; equilibrium existence is not convergence or welfare assurance.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/14`.

### AMA-T016 — Human override versus useful agent independence

**Id:** AMA-T016

**Name:** Human override versus useful agent independence

**Scope a:** A bounded autonomous system can act faster or more consistently than repeated manual choices.

**Scope b:** Humans may retain legitimate refusal, cancellation and accountability powers.

**Failure if a universalised:** Autonomous persistence continues after revocation or deprives affected people of usable control.

**Failure if b universalised:** Nominal human approval is required everywhere despite poor readiness and no meaningful ability to intervene.

**Bounded synthesis:** Make the specific approval/refusal/stop decision effective at the action boundary; define non-response and irreversible-effect handling without assuming human infallibility.

**Unresolved remainder:** The frozen evidence does not establish that adding a human always improves assurance.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/15`.

### AMA-T017 — Higher assurance versus open-ended generative behaviour

**Id:** AMA-T017

**Name:** Higher assurance versus open-ended generative behaviour

**Scope a:** Scoped models, executable checks and protected tests can justify claims over constrained behaviour.

**Scope b:** Generated plans and adaptive communication may handle tasks not covered by a fixed plan library.

**Failure if a universalised:** Unnecessary hard constraints or exhaustive-path demands remove useful authorised capability and become disproportionate.

**Failure if b universalised:** Fluent generated behaviour escapes the assumed model, tool authority or observation contract.

**Bounded synthesis:** Use bounded symbolic/typed interfaces, actual authority checks and version-sensitive tests; expose the unsupported open-ended remainder.

**Unresolved remainder:** Formal or simulation guarantees do not automatically cover arbitrary generated plans, new protocols or future environments.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/16`.

### AMA-T018 — Legal protocol completion versus actual willingness and execution

**Id:** AMA-T018

**Name:** Legal protocol completion versus actual willingness and execution

**Scope a:** Enactability and completion possibility can exclude important protocol dead ends in the defined model.

**Scope b:** Eventual fulfilment additionally depends on scheduling, willingness, capability, environment and adequate effect evidence.

**Failure if a universalised:** A legal path is advertised as an eventual real-world outcome although no actor takes it.

**Failure if b universalised:** Discarding useful protocol checks because they are not full execution assurance leaves avoidable structural deadlocks.

**Bounded synthesis:** Report possibility, assumed/established progress and accepted consequence separately; add the relevant liveness premises only when warranted.

**Unresolved remainder:** Open agents can refuse or disappear; a protocol cannot prove willingness outside its model.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/17`.

### AMA-T019 — Implementation abstraction versus operating-environment validity

**Id:** AMA-T019

**Name:** Implementation abstraction versus operating-environment validity

**Scope a:** A checked abstraction or semantic-preserving implementation mapping can support a precisely scoped claim.

**Scope b:** Actual observations, action effects, timing and participants can differ from the formal or simulated environment.

**Failure if a universalised:** Correct deliberation or generated code is treated as evidence of accurate sensing and successful actuation.

**Failure if b universalised:** Requiring a complete world model before any bounded guarantee rejects useful partial models and direct operations.

**Bounded synthesis:** Validate only the mappings and environmental premises material to the claimed effect, preserving limits and alternative implementations.

**Unresolved remainder:** Unobserved conditions, physical translation and changing deployment contexts may leave claims open.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/18`.

### AMA-T020 — Resilience versus fault-model boundaries

**Id:** AMA-T020

**Name:** Resilience versus fault-model boundaries

**Scope a:** Replication and fallback can preserve a specified computation or bounded service under their covered faults.

**Scope b:** Common-mode dependencies, arbitrary corruption or adversarial behaviour may lie outside the selected model.

**Failure if a universalised:** Decentralisation or replication is advertised as universal resilience and exact optimisation.

**Failure if b universalised:** Insisting on protection against every imaginable fault makes proportionate useful service infeasible.

**Bounded synthesis:** Name the covered failures and exact preserved property; monitor material assumptions where possible and provide explicit degradation or suspension.

**Unresolved remainder:** FT-DCOP preservation of fault-free underlying behaviour does not strengthen approximate Max-Sum into global optimality or cover arbitrary missing premises.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/19`.

### AMA-T021 — Evidence reachability versus privacy and bounded disclosure

**Id:** AMA-T021

**Name:** Evidence reachability versus privacy and bounded disclosure

**Scope a:** A consequential decision may need evidence produced elsewhere.

**Scope b:** That evidence can be sensitive or inaccessible to the nominal decision maker under legitimate disclosure limits.

**Failure if a universalised:** Coordination exposes private state or data beyond the rights of the recipient.

**Failure if b universalised:** The only authorised decision is impossible because sufficient evidence cannot be disclosed or established.

**Bounded synthesis:** Use a sufficient permitted evidence interface, an entitled alternative consumer or an explicitly limited judgement; do not infer a fact from inability to inspect it.

**Unresolved remainder:** Some acceptance conditions are epistemically unreachable under the chosen authority/privacy configuration; composition does not guarantee a feasible solution.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/20`.

### AMA-T022 — Cross-lineage convergence versus false independent corroboration

**Id:** AMA-T022

**Name:** Cross-lineage convergence versus false independent corroboration

**Scope a:** Several lineages can illuminate different bearers and criticisms of a shared mechanism.

**Scope b:** They may reuse the same paper, edition, benchmark, institution or empirical programme.

**Failure if a universalised:** Three citations to one result are reported as three independent confirmations or whole-family validation.

**Failure if b universalised:** Over-correcting for dependence discards genuine distinct claim roles or editions that must remain traceable.

**Bounded synthesis:** Retain all 234 local source records and exact access scopes while recording work/version and programme relationships; convergence improves interpretation, not automatic confidence.

**Unresolved remainder:** The corpus does not establish one global independent-study count or independently replicated whole-AMA benefit.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/21`.

### AMA-T023 — Risk-sensitive assurance versus control machinery cost

**Id:** AMA-T023

**Name:** Risk-sensitive assurance versus control machinery cost

**Scope a:** Consequential failures can justify targeted checks, trace custody and adversarial testing.

**Scope b:** Those controls consume resources, introduce new failure paths and can outlive their consumers.

**Failure if a universalised:** Verification and coordination become an end in themselves and delay the protected work.

**Failure if b universalised:** Cost reduction removes the only protection against an unacceptable failure or live obligation.

**Bounded synthesis:** Tie each control to a material failure/consumer and assess cheaper substitutes; preserve the obligation while permitting machinery retirement.

**Unresolved remainder:** No universal scalar cost/risk ranking or globally cheapest architecture is established by the frozen sources.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/22`.

### AMA-T024 — Useful lifecycle composition versus unsupported whole-method benefit

**Id:** AMA-T024

**Name:** Useful lifecycle composition versus unsupported whole-method benefit

**Scope a:** Compatible method and operational mechanisms can expose obligations missed by one local engineering view.

**Scope b:** That analytical coverage argument does not identify the causal net lifecycle benefit of the whole assembled family.

**Failure if a universalised:** An impressive union of methods is declared superior without a credible whole-intervention comparator.

**Failure if b universalised:** Absence of universal superiority evidence is misread as proof that every bounded component or configuration is useless.

**Bounded synthesis:** Retain conditional mechanism-level value, conventional exits and unresolved whole-method claims; do not turn this synthesis into a mandatory methodology mode.

**Unresolved remainder:** EAOSE-C119 and C120 remain separate unresolved intervention-scoped questions; whole Evolved-AMA superiority is likewise not established.

**Evidence boundary:** Analytical tension between preserved guarded criteria/counterclaims, not a measured trade-off curve or universal balance. Frozen source/model/access limits remain in each referenced property.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_TENSION_LEDGER.json#/tensions/23`.


## All 24 historical counterexamples

These are the September public source records, not the corrected r18 scientific findings. Source: `EVOLVED_AMA_ANALYTICAL_COUNTEREXAMPLES.json#/counterexamples` in the [immutable historical packet](https://github.com/theislampill/IMPLEMENTAUDIT.md/blob/cacfe97da7656b472bf4f11e50c9d106a6c3f92a/docs/research/genealogy/ama/packet/EVOLVED_AMA_FROZEN_PACKET.zip).

### AMA-X001 — Executable local plan and legal interaction, but unauthorised consequential action

**Id:** AMA-X001

**Name:** Executable local plan and legal interaction, but unauthorised consequential action

**Locally satisfied properties:** property: EAA:EAA-017; precise_local_pass: The local model contains a feasible refund plan and accurately records that the available tool can perform it; no execution authorisation is claimed by planning.; property: EMAS:EMAS-011; precise_local_pass: The interaction model permits the corresponding request and a completion path.; property: EAOSE:EAOSE-C020; precise_local_pass: The role model correctly distinguishes a drafting clerk from a supervisor with refund authority.

**Composition level failure:** The clerk’s agent directly executes the refund without supervisor authority and treats a successful tool effect as accepted organisational completion. Correct planning, protocol possibility and a correct role model do not warrant that act.

**Inherited criteria rejecting failure:** EAA:EAA-004; EAA:EAA-022; EMAS:EMAS-004; EMAS:EMAS-071; EAOSE:EAOSE-C006; EAOSE:EAOSE-C007; EAOSE:EAOSE-C098; EAOSE:EAOSE-C086

**Assumptions and scope:** Available capability is distinct from permission; the local checks listed are genuinely narrower than the omitted point-of-use and acceptance checks. No claim that all 278 properties hold.

**Novelty reason:** The stated global failure is already rejected by the listed inherited criterion or guarded inherited relation bundle. A new configuration, relation or failure witness is not by itself a new property identity.

**Evidence class:** ANALYTICAL_CONSTRUCTED_COUNTEREXAMPLE_NOT_EMPIRICAL

**Empirical validation:** false

**Local satisfaction warning:** Only the explicitly described local/projected checks are satisfied. No claim is made that all stronger inherited/global criteria hold; those identified as rejecting the failure are not silently counted as locally passed.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_ANALYTICAL_COUNTEREXAMPLES.json#/counterexamples/0`.

### AMA-X002 — Local intention retired while public promise remains live

**Id:** AMA-X002

**Name:** Local intention retired while public promise remains live

**Locally satisfied properties:** property: EAA:EAA-013; precise_local_pass: A locally adopted intention is dropped on correctly recognised loss of local relevance.; property: EMAS:EMAS-025; precise_local_pass: The public commitment ledger correctly shows an activated undischarged promise to a creditor.; property: EAOSE:EAOSE-C020; precise_local_pass: The original responsible role and its permission limits remain correctly modelled.

**Composition level failure:** The actor treats its local intention change as cancellation of the public promise and stops without notification or a valid release/transfer.

**Inherited criteria rejecting failure:** EMAS:EMAS-023; EMAS:EMAS-026; EMAS:EMAS-027; EAOSE:EAOSE-C051; EAOSE:EAOSE-C105

**Assumptions and scope:** The local goal is revocable, but the separately activated public obligation is not cancelled by that private event. This is distinct-bearer consistency, not a contradiction inside one goal variable.

**Novelty reason:** The stated global failure is already rejected by the listed inherited criterion or guarded inherited relation bundle. A new configuration, relation or failure witness is not by itself a new property identity.

**Evidence class:** ANALYTICAL_CONSTRUCTED_COUNTEREXAMPLE_NOT_EMPIRICAL

**Empirical validation:** false

**Local satisfaction warning:** Only the explicitly described local/projected checks are satisfied. No claim is made that all stronger inherited/global criteria hold; those identified as rejecting the failure are not silently counted as locally passed.

Exact original selector: `EVOLVED_AMA_ANALYTICAL_COUNTEREXAMPLES.json#/counterexamples/1`.

### AMA-X005 — Every local contribution completes, yet collective acceptance fails
